## Verdict: NO-GO

`mfl-h11-id` is not runnable as currently wired, and it does not yet prove that the expected plugin binary was loaded.

1. **P0 — The custom sideload replaces uv sync; the fresh worker has no virtual environment.** `--pjrt-wheel` creates a nonempty `worker_setup_scripts` tuple ([launch_diagnostics.py:289](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/launch_diagnostics.py:289)), which reaches `setup_scripts` ([train.py:1270](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:1270), [dispatch.py:79](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:79)). In Fray/Iris, a list runs verbatim and suppresses the default uv-sync script ([types.py:593](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/fray/src/fray/types.py:593), [Iris types.py:787](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/iris/src/iris/cluster/types.py:787)). The bundle explicitly excludes `.venv` ([bundle.py:32](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/iris/src/iris/cluster/client/bundle.py:32)), but the sideload’s first Python command is `"$IRIS_VENV/bin/python"` ([pjrt_sideload.py:26](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/pjrt_sideload.py:26)). It should therefore fail in setup before downloading the wheel. The specific feared ordering bug—locked PJRT being reinstalled after sideload—does **not** occur: Iris appends only cloudpickle/py-spy/memray ([setup_scripts.py:188](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/iris/src/iris/cluster/setup_scripts.py:188)), not uv sync or PJRT. The blocker is the missing preceding sync.

2. **P0 — No current log line proves which `.so` the identity process loaded.** `sideloaded jax-cuda13-pjrt 0.11.1+marin.ce6db0d2c555` ([pjrt_sideload.py:47](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/pjrt_sideload.py:47)) proves installed metadata at setup time, not a loaded binary. Runtime verification silently accepts any `0.11.1+marin.*` ([train.py:256](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:256)). The XLA line `Device kernel: ... cta_count=...` proves treatment engagement—592 conclusively excludes stock—but `cta_count=1184` is identical for stock and identity, so it cannot attest the identity wheel. The exact upstream line is [ragged_all_to_all_thunk.cc:978-985](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/backends/gpu/runtime/ragged_all_to_all_thunk.cc#L978-L985). Identity needs a runtime-process line naming the exact distribution version plus resolved `xla_cuda_plugin.so` path/hash before JAX initializes.

3. **P0 — The CTA knob is not carried across the first submission boundary.** The dispatcher correctly forwards every `XLA_*` variable it sees ([dispatch.py:24](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/dispatch.py:24)), but the remote coordinator only receives the explicit `-e` values in [arm.sh:89](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/arm.sh:89); that list contains `XLA_FLAGS`, not `XLA_RAGGED_A2A_DK_CTAS_PER_SM` ([arm.sh:101](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/arm.sh:101)). Merely setting the knob in the shell that invokes `arm.sh` will not reach the coordinator, hence all treatments can silently run at 8. Once explicitly injected into the coordinator, propagation is sound: Iris gives every task the same env, and each supervisor child copies the complete parent environment before adding rank fields ([multigpu_main.py:121](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/iris/src/iris/hooks/multigpu_main.py:121)). All 16×4 processes then inherit the same value.

4. **P1 — A cross-rank mismatch primarily hangs; it should not silently corrupt this invocation, but classification is generic.** Each CTA enters a world/LSA barrier indexed by `blockIdx.x` before doing any copy, and repeats the barrier after copying ([pinned kernel:199-231](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92/xla/stream_executor/gpu/ragged_all_to_all_device_kernel_lib.cu.h#L199-L231)). A rank with extra CTA IDs waits for peers that never launch those IDs. Communicator setup might reject mismatched requirements and crash earlier; otherwise the expected result is an indefinite first-barrier wait, not successful numerical corruption. The in-process progress watchdog is disabled because diagnostics inherit an empty `ProgressWatchdogConfig` ([hero_recipe.py:115](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/hero_recipe.py:115)). The external loop watchdog will usually report `WATCHDOG hard-cap compile-ceiling` ([watchdog.sh:99](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/watchdog.sh:99)); an early communicator/child abort becomes generic `WATCHDOG task-failed` ([watchdog.sh:138](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/watchdog.sh:138)). Neither identifies CTA mismatch.

5. **P1 — Artifact identity is weaker than the plan claims.** The branch is one commit ahead, but its repository diff contains two files: the header and removal of the workflow’s publish job; the latter should not alter wheel payload, but “one changed file” is false ([exact comparison](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/compare/c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92...ce6db0d2c555)). Pairing is otherwise strong: `config.json` pins JAX 0.11.1 and its exact JAX commit, and preflight verifies the pinned XLA base. The stock CUDA plugin’s `jax-cuda13-pjrt==0.11.1` dependency accepts the local `+marin.*` version ([plugin_setup.py:56-68](https://github.com/jax-ml/jax/blob/2d66622450e2c8633cda2307688ef7aa294bd6eb/jax_plugins/cuda/plugin_setup.py#L56-L68)); there is no runtime XLA-commit/build-date check. Remaining risks are material:

   - The workflow uses a moving Ubuntu runner and unpinned `apt-get install clang lld`, so rebuilt codegen can differ even at fixed sources.
   - Its “delta present” assertion only greps for `kGridSmMultiplier`, which already exists in the base, making that assertion vacuous ([build_pjrt.sh:63-67](https://github.com/marin-community/xla/blob/ce6db0d2c555/marin/build_pjrt.sh#L63-L67)).
   - Sideload accepts every `*.whl` in a mutable directory and verifies neither exactly one wheel nor an expected SHA-256 ([pjrt_sideload.py:35](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/pjrt_sideload.py:35), [pjrt_sideload.py:46](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/pjrt_sideload.py:46)).

   A `-0.1` identity result should freeze the sweep, but it does **not** establish build drift: it is below the declared ~0.17 decision bar and is only one draw. It should trigger a contemporaneous stock-wheel salted control plus a second identity draw and exact wheel/hash inspection. Identity-only regression then implicates the wheel/build; both moving together means reference drift.

6. **P1 — Do not pool pre-salt and salted draws for the CTAS=4 causal estimate.** Pre-salt `h92-a/b` are 23.680/23.779 ([results.tsv:15](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/results.tsv:15)); salted controls are 23.721/23.645 and changed peak memory by 0.75 GiB ([results.tsv:20](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/results.tsv:20)). Pre-salt draws may inform the historical noise bar, but not the treatment-control center. The clean keep rule is two CTAS=4 draws versus two wheel-matched, salted CTAS=8 controls—the identity draw and a fresh bracket—with `median(T4) − median(C8) > max(0.17, 3×contemporaneous run-SD)`, both treatment draws directionally consistent, CTA logs 592 versus 1184, and fidelity gates clean. One merely “non-negative” C4 draw is insufficient evidence for advancing to C2.

7. **P1 — `ragged_all_to_all_thunk=3` is too chatty for a ~0.17-MFU scored decision.** The model has 48 layers ([heuristic.py:94](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/heuristic.py:94)) and the training path nominally executes four ragged collectives per layer—forward dispatch/return plus their backward transposes ([ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:259](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:259), [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:297](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:297), [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:323](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:323), [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:352](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:352)). That is nominally 192 VLOGs/rank/step, about 12,288 lines across 64 ranks per step, before rematerialization effects. Hundreds of thousands of host log writes can add CPU/log-shipping pressure and variance. Because the log is per invocation, not once per process, enabling it uniformly does not make the perturbation harmless. Engagement should be collected outside the scored window/run or with once-per-process observability.
